  "content": "# Process & Automation Blueprint\n\n**Project:** orchestration-control-room\n**Version:** v0.1 | **Date:** 2026-07-22\n**BoK Applied:** BPM CBOK v4 (Process Modeling \u00b7 Analysis \u00b7 Performance Mgmt)\n**Status:** Complete\n\n---\n\n## 1. Process Inventory\n\n| ID | Processo | Tipo | Automa\u00e7\u00e3o |\n|----|----------|------|-----------|\n| **PROC-OCR-01** | Abrir a Sala e ler o estado operacional | Leitura | Polling client-side (sem cron) |\n| **PROC-OCR-02** | Criar / alternar uma Orquestra (workflow) | CRUD | Nenhuma (a\u00e7\u00e3o humana) |\n| **PROC-OCR-03** | Wire de n\u00f3s + persist\u00eancia do grafo | Edi\u00e7\u00e3o | Save debounced (client) |\n| **PROC-OCR-04** | Rodar um N\u00f3 Agente | Dispatch | Reusa `agent-task-execute` |\n| **PROC-OCR-05** | Port\u00e3o de Qualidade (N especialistas) | Gate | Reusa `agent-task-execute` \u00d7 N + bloqueio |\n| **PROC-OCR-06** | Publicar do fluxo (privado-primeiro) | Publish | Reusa `publish-space-asset` \u2192 `scheduled_posts` |\n\n> **Nenhum cron novo.** A opera\u00e7\u00e3o da Sala \u00e9 s\u00edncrona/interativa; os \u00fanicos crons envolvidos\n> (`auto-publish`, autopilot cadence, agent-daily-pulse) j\u00e1 existem e alimentam as fontes que a Vista l\u00ea.\n\n---\n\n## 2. BPMN-lite Process Maps\n\n### PROC-OCR-01 \u2014 Ver o coletivo (fluxo prim\u00e1rio do Sovereign)\n```\nSwimlanes:  Sovereign | OperationPanel | Hooks | DB (RLS-own)\n\n[Start: abre /dashboard/orchestration]\n   \u2502\n   \u25bc (Sovereign)\n[Aba \"Opera\u00e7\u00e3o\" ativa]\n   \u2502\n   \u25bc (Hooks \u2014 em paralelo)\n \u250c\u2500 useOperationOverview \u2192 SELECT crew_agents + agent_executions \u2500\u2500\u2510\n \u251c\u2500 useOperationPending  \u2192 SELECT scheduled_posts + autopilot_cycles \u2524 (todos RLS-own)\n \u2514\u2500 useCollectiveImpact  \u2192 SELECT collective_efficiency_ledger \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n   \u2502\n   \u25bc (OperationPanel)\n[Render: roster (ativo/idle/pendente/erro) \u00b7 pend\u00eancias \u00b7 impacto]\n   \u2502\n   \u25bc (gateway) \u2014 algum agente parado que deveria trabalhar?\n   \u251c\u2500 N\u00e3o \u2192 [monitora] \u2500\u2500(refetch ~12s)\u2500\u2500\u25b6 volta ao render\n   \u2514\u2500 Sim \u2192 dispara PROC-OCR-04 (N\u00f3 Agente)\n[End]\n```\n**SLA:** primeiro render < 3s; lat\u00eancia de reflexo de mudan\u00e7a \u2264 1 intervalo de poll (~12s).\n\n### PROC-OCR-02 \u2014 Criar/alternar Orquestra\n```\nSwimlanes:  Sovereign | WorkflowSelector | useWorkflows | workflows(DB)\n\n[Start] \u2192 [clica \"+ Nova Orquestra\"] \u2192 [informa name + goal]\n   \u2192 useWorkflows.create \u2192 INSERT workflows (user_id = auth.uid())  [RLS WITH CHECK]\n   \u2192 [lista invalidada] \u2192 [seleciona o novo] \u2192 store.setCurrentWorkflow(id)\n   \u2192 useOrchestrationGraph carrega workflows.graph \u2192 canvas hidratado\n[End]\n\nGateway de erro: INSERT negado pela RLS (user_id != auth.uid()) \u2192 toast pt-BR, nenhum estado alterado.\n```\n\n### PROC-OCR-04 \u2014 Rodar um N\u00f3 Agente (dispatch, reuse)\n```\nSwimlanes:  Sovereign | AgentNode | agent-task-execute(edge) | DB\n\n[Start] \u2192 [escolhe agente/diretor no dropdown (crew_agents)] \u2192 [Rodar]\n   \u2192 invoke agent-task-execute { agent_id, task_prompt, idempotency_key }\n   \u2192 (edge) auth.getUser(JWT)\n   \u2192 (edge) deduct_mco_coins (n\u00e3o-Sovereign) [AT\u00d4MICO]\n   \u2192 (edge) dispatch groq/BYOK\n   \u2192 (edge) INSERT agent_executions {status, capability_key, result_summary, cost_mcoin}\n   \u2192 n\u00f3: running \u2192 done|error\n[End] \u2192 PROC-OCR-01 reflete no pr\u00f3ximo poll\n```\n\n### PROC-OCR-05 \u2014 Port\u00e3o de Qualidade (N especialistas)\n```\n[Start] \u2192 [multi-select especialistas] \u2192 [Rodar gate]\n   \u2192 parallel( para cada especialista: agent-task-execute ) [fan-out]\n   \u2192 coleta veredictos\n   \u2192 gateway: algum reprovou?\n       \u251c\u2500 Sim \u2192 n\u00f3 = blocked; arestas de sa\u00edda N\u00c3O animam; downstream n\u00e3o roda\n       \u2514\u2500 N\u00e3o \u2192 n\u00f3 = done; libera downstream\n[End]\n```\n\n### PROC-OCR-06 \u2014 Publicar do fluxo (privado-primeiro)\n```\n[Start] \u2192 [conecta output upstream ao N\u00f3 Publicar] \u2192 [escolhe redes]\n   \u2192 para cada rede: resolve PublicationFormat (format-specs) \u2192 PublicationPreview (mockup fiel)\n   \u2192 gateway: availability(format)?\n       \u251c\u2500 preview \u2192 bot\u00e3o publish desabilitado (\"Em breve\")\n       \u2514\u2500 real \u2192 [a\u00e7\u00e3o default: Salvar rascunho (privado)]\n                   \u2192 (opcional, gated GO) [Publicar] \u2192 usePublishSpaceAsset\n                        \u2192 publish-space-asset (owner-scoped) \u2192 INSERT scheduled_posts (queued)\n                        \u2192 auto-publish (cron existente) drena\n[End]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Orchestration Integration Points\n\n| Ponto | Integra com | Novo? |\n|-------|-------------|:-----:|\n| N\u00f3 Agente \u2192 `agent-task-execute` | dispatch de agente (groq/BYOK), cobran\u00e7a at\u00f4mica | N\u00e3o (reuse) |\n| N\u00f3 Publicar \u2192 `publish-space-asset` \u2192 `scheduled_posts` \u2192 `auto-publish` | rail de publica\u00e7\u00e3o social | N\u00e3o (reuse) |\n| Vista \u2192 `collective_efficiency_ledger` | medi\u00e7\u00e3o do coletivo (agentic-governance) | N\u00e3o (reuse) |\n| Trigger/Run do canvas \u2192 `orchestrate-content` | pipeline de campanha (existente) | N\u00e3o (reuse, inalterado) |\n| Ponto de dedu\u00e7\u00e3o de mcoCoins | **s\u00f3** dentro de `agent-task-execute` / rails reusados | **Nenhum novo** |\n\n---\n\n## 4. Error Handling & Retry Strategy\n\n| Processo | Falha | Recupera\u00e7\u00e3o |\n|----------|-------|-------------|\n| PROC-OCR-01 | Query de leitura falha | Fail-soft: painel mostra estado degradado + retry autom\u00e1tico do TanStack; nunca quebra a tela |\n| PROC-OCR-02 | INSERT/UPDATE workflow negado (RLS) | Toast pt-BR; estado local revertido; nenhuma escrita parcial |\n| PROC-OCR-03 | Save do grafo falha | Debounce re-tenta; localStorage mant\u00e9m c\u00f3pia de fallback (OTD-OCR-001) |\n| PROC-OCR-04 | `agent-task-execute` retorna erro/402 | N\u00f3 \u2192 `error`; toast; **sem** cobran\u00e7a (RPC at\u00f4mico n\u00e3o debita em falha); `idempotency_key` evita duplo d\u00e9bito no retry |\n| PROC-OCR-05 | Um especialista falha (n\u00e3o reprova, erra) | Pol\u00edtica: falha de execu\u00e7\u00e3o \u2260 reprova\u00e7\u00e3o \u2192 n\u00f3 `error` (distinto de `blocked`); log; permite re-rodar |\n| PROC-OCR-06 | Publish real falha | `scheduled_posts.status='failed'` + `error_message` (rail existente); rascunho preservado; retry via rail |\n\n---\n\n## 5. SLA Definitions\n\n| Etapa | Dura\u00e7\u00e3o esperada | Timeout |\n|-------|------------------|---------|\n| Primeiro render da Vista | < 3s | \u2014 (fail-soft) |\n| Reflexo de mudan\u00e7a na Vista | \u2264 ~12s (1 poll) | \u2014 |\n| CRUD de workflow | < 500ms (INSERT/UPDATE simples) | padr\u00e3o Supabase |\n| Dispatch N\u00f3 Agente | herda SLA de `agent-task-execute` | herdado |\n| Preview do N\u00f3 Publicar | instant\u00e2neo (display-only, 0 rede) | \u2014 |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Cron Jobs\n\n**Nenhum cron novo.** Crons existentes que alimentam as fontes da Vista (n\u00e3o modificados):\n`auto-publish` (drena `scheduled_posts`), cadence do viral-autopilot (escreve `autopilot_cycles`),\n`agent-daily-pulse` (escreve `agent_executions`). A Sala \u00e9 **observadora** desses; n\u00e3o os agenda.\n\n---\n\n_Generated by MCORCH BoK Scribe Agent v1.0.0_\n"
}

I'm mapping out the Pattern Conformance Declaration by filling in one row for each of the 21 canonical patterns, and I'm identifying which ones this module touches—Orchestration connects to Multi-Agent and Prompt Chaining, Human-in-the-Loop handles the Quality Gate, Tool Use manages Agent node dispatch, and Memory/Learning impacts the collective efficiency ledger through Reflection.

Now I'm working through each pattern systematically: Prompt Chaining is definitely present since the canvas wires nodes into a directed acyclic graph (trigger→agent→gate→publish), and runPipeline calls orchestrate-content which implements that chaining. Routing happens when the Agent node dropdown dispatches tasks to specific crew agents based on the capability key. Parallelization occurs at the Quality Gate where N specialist reviewers fan out in parallel. Reflection is embedded in the Quality Gate's review process.

Tool Use is marked yes since both the Agent node and Publish node invoke real edge functions like agent-task-execute and publish-space-asset. For Planning, the workflow graph itself is a human-authored plan rather than autonomously generated, so I'm marking this as deferred and pointing to OTD-AG-005 for dynamic re-planning capabilities.

Multi-Agent coordination is definitely yes — the whole architecture orchestrates the Pantheon/Constellation collective with quality gates involving multiple specialists, and Nó Agente dispatches agents across the system. For Memory Management, workflows persist the graph and goals through config, and the Vista reads from the collective_efficiency_ledger which feeds into the mesh, with BoK seals writing to mcorch_nodes, though the workflows table itself lacks embeddings. ective gets durable, per-tenant memory of workflow state and reads the ledger's derived performance history—that's legitimate, so I'll mark `yes` with honest framing (persistent operational state plus seal to mesh), though it's config-memory rather than semantic recall. For Learning & Adaptation, the Vista surfaces ledger signals for human adaptation but there's no closed RL loop, so I'm marking that `deferred` and linking it to the same ticket.

Model Context Protocol doesn't apply here. Goal Setting & Monitoring is core though—workflows.goal literally defines per-workflow goals and the Vista Operacional monitors who's working and impact versus targets, so that's a `yes`. Exception Handling & Recovery covers fail-soft hooks, Quality Gate blocks, retry via idempotency, and failed posts routing, marking that `yes` as well.

Human-in-the-Loop is fundamental—the entire surface is humans commanding the collective, Quality Gate enforces it, and privado-primeiro requires human approval to publish. Knowledge Retrieval doesn't apply since the module writes to the mesh but doesn't retrieve semantically at runtime. Inter-Agent Communication lacks a formal A2A protocol; coordination happens through the orchestration layer rather than direct agent-to-agent messaging. Atomic billing covers the resource optimization requirement. There's no custom reasoning framework, so that's not applicable. The safety layer is solid with role-based access control, private-first design, and inherited protections against server-side request forgery. The Vista and Quality Gate serve as the evaluation and monitoring system by reading the ledger. Prioritization is handled at the user level through workflow activation, which I'll mark as deferred pending a formal prioritization schema.

The module is operational rather than exploratory, so discovery isn't a factor. Tallying up the capabilities: chaining, routing, parallelization, reflection, tool use, multi-agent coordination, memory, goal tracking, exception handling, human-in-the-loop, resource awareness, guardrails, and evaluation all register as implemented—that's thirteen core capabilities.

Four more are deferred to other tickets: planning, learning, agent-to-agent communication, and prioritization scheduling. Another four don't apply here: MCP, RAG, reasoning, and exploration. That brings the total to twenty-one.

Thirteen yes answers might seem high, but this is genuinely an orchestration control room focused on execution, not just measurement like the agentic-governance module was. Still, I should double-check the borderline cases to make sure I'm not inflating the count.

For prompt chaining, it's a solid yes—the canvas wires nodes into a DAG and runPipeline orchestrates the content through reuse of orchestrate-content. On memory, it's trickier. The workflows.graph holds durable config state, the ledger captures the collective's performance memory that Vista reads, and seal writes to GraphRAG. I can mark it yes, but I need to be precise in the explanation: it's config and operational memory plus derived ledger reads, not semantic recall through embeddings.

Exception handling is clearly yes with the block-on-fail gate, fail-soft behavior, idempotency, and refund rails. Resource awareness is also yes since it surfaces mco and resultado from the ledger. Looking at this holistically, thirteen yes marks for an orchestration control room that touches the collective, monitoring, human-in-the-loop, tool-use, and reflection is defensible—the prompt itself explicitly calls out these touchpoints.

For routing and parallelization, I want to be more precise. Routing is definitely yes because the agent dispatch routes tasks to chosen agents and capability_key routes to target functions. Parallelization is also yes since the Quality Gate fans out to N specialists in parallel, though it's marked as a deferred Fatia 3 feature. Since this is a new module where nothing's built yet, everything here is design-declared rather than implemented.

The key distinction is that Pattern Conformance is a declaration made at SDD time, before any code exists. For a brand-new module, marking something as "yes" means the design embodies the pattern with a cited FR—the artifact is the FR and design itself, not production code. The agentic-governance example marked patterns as yes because that module was already live, but for a pre-code BoK, the convention is that yes means the module encarna the pattern with a design reference. It's a declaration, not a sign-off.

Now I'm reconsidering whether Planning should be deferred or marked as n-a. The user authors a workflow graph through the surface, which is human planning, not agentic planning. The Planning pattern is about the agent generating plans, and this module doesn't do that agentically.

I'll mark it as deferred with OTD-AG-005 (dynamic re-planning) and note honestly that the module holds human-authored plans while agentic plan-generation and re-planning on divergence are deferred.

For A2A, the Quality Gate coordinates multiple agents but through hub-mediated control room rather than peer-to-peer messaging, so I'll mark that deferred too with OTD-AG-006.

Now I'm ready to write the declaration file following the master template structure with the 21-row table, scoreboard, and verification gates.

</details>

**`workflows` DDL specified** (the ONLY new table — mirrors `vm_canvas_projects`/`spaces`):
```sql
CREATE TABLE public.workflows (
  id uuid PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  user_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  name text NOT NULL CHECK (char_length(name) BETWEEN 1 AND 120),
  goal text CHECK (goal IS NULL OR char_length(goal) <= 500),
  status text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active' CHECK (status IN ('active','archived')),
  graph jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{"nodes":[],"edges":[]}',
  viewport jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{"x":0,"y":0,"zoom":1}',
  is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
-- RLS default-deny + 4 own-policies (select/insert/update/delete auth.uid()=user_id)
-- indexes: (user_id, updated_at DESC); partial (user_id) WHERE is_active
-- NO embedding column (config, not knowledge) → no HNSW, no embed-mcorch-node trigger
```
